99	export function mount(root, ctx) {
100	  const { store, loop, signal } = ctx;
101	  const article = root.closest('article') ?? root;
102	  const facade = paramFacade();
103	  let t = 0, steps = 0;           // this pane's clock: the tuple's t belongs to the players
104	  let running = false, carry = 0;
105	  let view = null, frozenMap = null;
106	
107	  // ---- the force rows: switch, equation, and whether it counts toward the sum. Each row's
108	  // off-hint is always in the DOM at its full width (CSS hides it by :has(input:checked), not
109	  // a text swap) and the row itself has a reserved min-height, so toggling a force or
110	  // dragging a number wide never wraps the row and shoves the stage below it around. ----
111	  const rows = scene.get().forces.map((f, i) => el('div', { class: 'transport force-row' },
112	    toggleFn({
113	      label: f.type[0].toUpperCase() + f.type.slice(1),
114	      get: () => scene.get().forces[i].on, set: v => scene.toggleForce(i, v), subscribe: scene.subscribe, signal,
115	    }),
116	    fragment(EQUATIONS[f.type]),
117	    el('span', { class: 'muted force-off-hint' }, 'off — not in ΣF'),
118	  ));
119	  const runBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button', title: 'Run explicit Euler steps of the tuple’s h', 'aria-pressed': 'false' }, 'Run');
120	  const stepBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button', title: 'One explicit Euler step of the tuple’s h' }, 'Step');
121	  const resetBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button' }, 'Reset');
122	  root.append(controls(rows, el('div', { class: 'transport' }, el('div', { class: 'transport-group' }, runBtn, stepBtn, resetBtn))));
123	
124	  const stage = createStage(root, { layers: ['plane'], aspect: 'wide', signal });
125	  root.append(el('div', { class: 'totals' }, fragment(TOTALS)));
126	
127	  const mapNow = () => frozenMap ?? arrowMap(scene.get());
128	
129	  stage.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
130	    const s = scene.get();
131	    const { body } = s;
132	    const { list, sum } = forceVectors(s);
133	    const map = mapNow();
134	    const a = [sum[0] / body.m, sum[1] / body.m];
135	    const g = stage.ctx('plane');
136	    view = makeView({ w, h, dpr, halfW: HALF_W });
137	    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
138	    drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 'x', yLabel: 'y' });
139	
140	    // the spring's anchor is part of the picture: the spring pulls back toward the origin
141	    const spring = s.forces.find(f => f.type === 'spring');
142	    if (spring?.on && mag(body.x) > 1e-6) {
143	      g.save(); g.strokeStyle = cssVar(FORCE_COLOR.spring); g.globalAlpha = 0.35; g.lineWidth = dpr; g.setLineDash([3 * dpr, 3 * dpr]);
144	      g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(view.X(0), view.Y(0)); g.lineTo(view.X(body.x[0]), view.Y(body.x[1])); g.stroke(); g.restore();
145	      drawText(g, view, 'spring anchor', 0, 0, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 10, dx: 6, dy: 14 });
146	    }
147	
148	    // a dotted preview of the next FORECAST_STEPS of this same step, from wherever the body
149	    // is right now: it redraws on every scene change, so it is always what happens next
150	    const preview = forecast(s, store.get().h, FORECAST_STEPS);
151	    drawPolyline(g, view, preview.map(p => p[0]), preview.map(p => p[1]),
152	      { color: cssVar('--muted'), width: 1.5, dash: [2, 3], alpha: 0.8 });
153	
154	    for (const f of list) drawForceArrow(g, view, body.x, f.F, map, { color: FORCE_COLOR[f.type], label: f.label });
155	    drawForceArrow(g, view, body.x, sum, map, { color: SUM_COLOR, width: 4, head: 11 });
156	    drawForceArrow(g, view, body.x, a, map, { color: ACCEL_COLOR, width: 1.5, head: 6 });
157	    if (mag(body.v) > 1e-6) drawForceArrow(g, view, body.x, body.v, map, { color: '--stable', width: 1.5, head: 6, alpha: 0.8 });
158	    drawPoint(g, view, body.x[0], body.x[1], { r: BODY_R, fill: cssVar('--fg') });
159	
160	    // a legend for the three arrows that share the body's tail (and, when m = 1, a tip)
161	    drawText(g, view, 'ΣF', view.xMin, view.yMax, { color: cssVar(SUM_COLOR), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: 16 });
162	    drawText(g, view, 'a = ΣF / m', view.xMin, view.yMax, { color: cssVar(ACCEL_COLOR), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: 30 });
163	    drawText(g, view, 'v', view.xMin, view.yMax, { color: cssVar('--stable'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: 44 });
164	    drawText(g, view, `⋯ next ${FORECAST_STEPS} steps`, view.xMin, view.yMax, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: 58 });
165	
166	    // the clock and the state the equations do not already carry
167	    const hint = list.length === 0 ? 'every force is off' : 'arrow lengths are compressed (log) so all four fit; the equations are the truth';
168	    drawText(g, view, `x = (${fmt(body.x[0], 2)}, ${fmt(body.x[1], 2)})   v = (${fmt(body.v[0], 2)}, ${fmt(body.v[1], 2)})`,
169	      view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: -32 });
170	    drawText(g, view, `t = ${fmt(t, 3)} s: ${steps} step${steps === 1 ? '' : 's'} of h = ${fmt(store.get().h, 3)} s${running ? '   running' : ''}`,
171	      view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: -18 });
172	    drawText(g, view, hint, view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 10, dx: 8, dy: -5 });
173	  });
174	
175	  // ---- drags: the body, and every arrow tip long enough to grab ----
176	  const canvas = stage.canvas('plane');
177	  createDragHandles(canvas, {
178	    signal, hitRadius: 12,
179	    handles: () => {
180	      const s = scene.get();
181	      const map = mapNow();
182	      const hs = [{ id: 'body', x: s.body.x[0], y: s.body.x[1] }];
183	      for (const f of forceVectors(s).list) {
184	        const d = map.toPlot(f.F);
185	        if (mag(d) < 0.15) continue;   // too short to grab; the body wins the hit test
186	        hs.push({ id: f.i, x: s.body.x[0] + d[0], y: s.body.x[1] + d[1] });
187	      }
188	      return hs;
189	    },
190	    view: () => view ?? makeView({ w: 1, h: 1, dpr: 1, halfW: HALF_W }),
191	    onStart: () => { frozenMap = arrowMap(scene.get()); },
192	    onMove: (id, p) => {
193	      if (id === 'body') { scene.moveBody(Math.max(-HALF_W, Math.min(HALF_W, p.x)), Math.max(-HALF_H, Math.min(HALF_H, p.y))); return; }
194	      const s = scene.get();
195	      const f = s.forces[id];
196	      const F = forceVectors(s).list.find(v => v.i === id)?.F ?? [0, 0];
197	      const Fnext = (frozenMap ?? arrowMap(s)).toForce([p.x - s.body.x[0], p.y - s.body.x[1]]);
198	      const patch = scalePatch(f, F, Fnext);
199	      if (patch) scene.paramStore(id).set(patch);
200	    },
201	    onEnd: () => { frozenMap = null; stage.invalidate(); },
202	  });
203	
204	  // ---- step time: one explicit Euler step in 2D, by hand or run continuously ----
205	  function step() {
206	    const s = scene.get();
207	    const h = store.get().h;
208	    const a = netForce(s).map(F => F / s.body.m);
209	    const { x, v } = s.body;
210	    scene.moveBody(x[0] + h * v[0], x[1] + h * v[1]);
211	    scene.setVelocity(v[0] + h * a[0], v[1] + h * a[1]);
212	    t += h; steps++;
213	    stage.invalidate();
214	  }
215	  function reset() {
216	    const body = defaultScene().body;   // the displaced, moving body: drag and spring are not zero vectors
217	    scene.setVelocity(body.v[0], body.v[1]);
218	    scene.moveBody(body.x[0], body.x[1]);
219	    t = 0; steps = 0;
220	    stage.invalidate();
221	  }
222	  function setRunning(v) {
223	    running = v; carry = 0;
224	    runBtn.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(running));
225	    runBtn.textContent = running ? 'Pause' : 'Run';
226	    stage.invalidate();
227	  }
228	  const offFrame = loop.onFrame(dt => {
229	    if (!running) return;
230	    carry += dt;
231	    const h = store.get().h;
232	    let n = Math.min(Math.floor(carry / h), 200);
233	    carry -= n * h;
234	    while (n-- > 0) step();
235	  });
236	  runBtn.addEventListener('click', () => setRunning(!running), { signal });
237	  stepBtn.addEventListener('click', step, { signal });
238	  resetBtn.addEventListener('click', reset, { signal });
239	
240	  // ---- live math: every scrub and every slot reads the one facade over the scene ----
241	  bindScrub(article, facade, { signal });
242	  bindMath(article, facade, () => {
243	    const s = scene.get();
244	    const { list, sum } = forceVectors(s, { all: true });
245	    const at = type => list.find(v => v.type === type)?.F ?? [0, 0];
246	    const [Fgx, Fgy] = at('gravity'), [Fdx, Fdy] = at('drag'), [Fsx, Fsy] = at('spring');
247	    return {
248	      Fgx, Fgy, Fdx, Fdy, Fsx, Fsy,
249	      Sx: sum[0], Sy: sum[1],
250	      ax: sum[0] / s.body.m, ay: sum[1] / s.body.m,
251	    };
252	  }, { signal });
253	
254	  const unsub = scene.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
255	  const unsubTuple = store.subscribe((s, patch) => { if ('h' in patch) stage.invalidate(); }, { immediate: false });
256	  return {
257	    pause() { setRunning(false); },
258	    destroy() { offFrame(); unsub(); unsubTuple(); },
259	  };
260	}

1	// Panel 2, right: the rotational half, on the rigid body from panel 1's drill-down. The
2	// scene's forces are applied at one point of the body a lever arm d from its center; each
3	// makes a torque r × F, the torques sum to T = I θ″ with I = m (w² + h²) / 12 the moment of
4	// inertia of the rectangle, and θ is integrated locally (there is no rigid-body store; the
5	// scene holds a point mass). The translational sum does not depend on where the forces
6	// act; the torque does, which is the whole point of the lever arm d — a scrubbable number
7	// inside its own equation, "at d = …".
8	//
9	// θ, θ′, t and the lever arm live in a pane-local store so the equations under the stage can
10	// be ordinary live MathML: one bindMath over that store catches the integration, a second
11	// over the scene catches a force being toggled or dragged on the spine.
12	
13	import { el, fmt, fragment } from 'shared/dom.js';
14	import { createStore } from 'shared/state.js';
15	import { createStage } from 'shared/gfx/stage.js';
16	import { cssVar, makeView, drawGrid, drawText } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
17	import { scene } from 'shared/scene.js';
18	import { controls } from 'shared/ui/controls.js';
19	import { bindMath } from 'shared/ui/livemath.js';
20	import { bindScrub } from 'shared/ui/scrub.js';
21	import { FORCE_COLOR, SUM_COLOR, forceVectors, arrowMap, drawForceArrow } from './arrows.js';
22	
23	const BODY = { w: 1.6, h: 1 };
24	const HALF_W = 4;
25	const LEVER = [-0.8, 0.8];
26	
27	const EQUATIONS = `
28	<math display="block"><mrow>
29	  <munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo>
30	  <mi>r</mi><mo>×</mo><munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo>
31	  <mn data-var="T" data-digits="3">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mspace width="1em"/>
32	  <mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>w</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac>
33	  <mo>=</mo><mn data-var="I" data-digits="3">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mspace width="1em"/>
34	  <msup><mi>θ</mi><mo>″</mo></msup><mo>=</mo>
35	  <mfrac><mrow><munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><mi>I</mi></mfrac>
36	  <mo>=</mo><mn data-var="alpha" data-digits="3">0</mn>
37	</mrow></math>
38	<math display="block"><mrow>
39	  <munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo>
40	  <mo>(</mo><mn data-var="Sx" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn data-var="Sy" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>)</mo>
41	  <mspace width="1em"/><mtext>at</mtext><mspace width="0.4em"/>
42	  <mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn data-var="lever" data-scrub="lever" data-digits="2">0</mn>
43	</mrow></math>`;

45	export function mount(root, ctx) {
46	  const { store, loop, signal } = ctx;
47	  const article = root.closest('article') ?? root;
48	  let running = false, carry = 0;
49	
50	  // the body's rotation state and where the forces act: local to this pane
51	  const rot = createStore({ theta: 0, omega: 0, t: 0, lever: 0.5 }, {
52	    limits: { theta: [-Infinity, Infinity], omega: [-Infinity, Infinity], t: [0, Infinity], lever: LEVER },
53	    validate: () => undefined,
54	    presets: {},
55	  });
56	
57	  const stage = createStage(root, { layers: ['plane'], aspect: 'wide', signal });
58	  root.append(fragment(EQUATIONS));
59	
60	  /** The rotational sum at the current θ. */
61	  function torque() {
62	    const s = scene.get();
63	    const { theta, lever } = rot.get();
64	    const { list, sum } = forceVectors(s);
65	    const r = [lever * Math.cos(theta), lever * Math.sin(theta)];
66	    const I = s.body.m * (BODY.w ** 2 + BODY.h ** 2) / 12;
67	    const T = r[0] * sum[1] - r[1] * sum[0];
68	    return { list, sum, r, I, T, alpha: T / I, s };
69	  }
70	
71	  stage.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
72	    const { list, sum, r, T, alpha, s } = torque();
73	    const { theta, omega, t, lever } = rot.get();
74	    const { body } = s;
75	    const map = arrowMap(s);
76	    const g = stage.ctx('plane');
77	    const view = makeView({ w, h, dpr, halfW: HALF_W });
78	    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
79	    drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 'x', yLabel: 'y' });
80	
81	    // the body, rotated by θ about its center of mass
82	    g.save();
83	    g.translate(view.X(body.x[0]), view.Y(body.x[1]));
84	    g.rotate(-theta);
85	    const pw = BODY.w * view.sx, ph = BODY.h * view.sy;
86	    g.fillStyle = cssVar('--accent-soft'); g.strokeStyle = cssVar('--accent'); g.lineWidth = 2 * dpr;
87	    g.beginPath(); g.rect(-pw / 2, -ph / 2, pw, ph); g.fill(); g.stroke();
88	    g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(0, 0); g.lineTo(pw / 2, 0); g.stroke();
89	    g.fillStyle = cssVar('--accent');
90	    g.beginPath(); g.arc(0, 0, 3 * dpr, 0, Math.PI * 2); g.fill();
91	    g.restore();
92	
93	    // the lever arm and the point of application
94	    const at = [body.x[0] + r[0], body.x[1] + r[1]];
95	    g.save();
96	    g.strokeStyle = cssVar('--fg'); g.lineWidth = 2.5 * dpr;
97	    g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(view.X(body.x[0]), view.Y(body.x[1])); g.lineTo(view.X(at[0]), view.Y(at[1])); g.stroke();
98	    g.fillStyle = cssVar('--fg');
99	    g.beginPath(); g.arc(view.X(at[0]), view.Y(at[1]), 5 * dpr, 0, Math.PI * 2); g.fill();
100	    g.restore();
101	    drawText(g, view, `r, d = ${fmt(lever, 2)}`, (body.x[0] + at[0]) / 2, (body.x[1] + at[1]) / 2, { color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 10, dx: 6, dy: -6 });
102	
103	    for (const f of list) drawForceArrow(g, view, at, f.F, map, { color: FORCE_COLOR[f.type], label: f.label });
104	    drawForceArrow(g, view, at, sum, map, { color: SUM_COLOR, width: 4, head: 11, label: 'ΣF' });
105	
106	    // the torque as an arc at the center: counter-clockwise for T > 0
107	    if (Math.abs(T) > 1e-6) {
108	      const R = 0.55 * view.sx, cx = view.X(body.x[0]), cy = view.Y(body.x[1]);
109	      const sweep = Math.min(Math.PI * 1.5, 0.4 + Math.abs(alpha) * 0.15);
110	      g.save();
111	      g.strokeStyle = g.fillStyle = cssVar('--unstable'); g.lineWidth = 2.5 * dpr;
112	      g.beginPath(); g.arc(cx, cy, R, -Math.PI / 2, -Math.PI / 2 + (T > 0 ? -sweep : sweep), T > 0); g.stroke();
113	      const end = -Math.PI / 2 + (T > 0 ? -sweep : sweep);
114	      const ex = cx + R * Math.cos(end), ey = cy + R * Math.sin(end);
115	      const tangent = end + (T > 0 ? -Math.PI / 2 : Math.PI / 2);
116	      const hd = 8 * dpr;
117	      g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(ex, ey);
118	      g.lineTo(ex - hd * Math.cos(tangent - 0.4), ey - hd * Math.sin(tangent - 0.4));
119	      g.lineTo(ex - hd * Math.cos(tangent + 0.4), ey - hd * Math.sin(tangent + 0.4));
120	      g.closePath(); g.fill();
121	      g.restore();
122	      drawText(g, view, 'T', body.x[0], body.x[1] + 0.55, { color: cssVar('--unstable'), size: 12, align: 'center', dx: T > 0 ? -14 : 14, dy: -6 });
123	    }
124	
125	    // the rotation state, and the point the lever arm is there to make
126	    drawText(g, view, `θ = ${fmt(theta, 3)} rad   θ′ = ${fmt(omega, 3)} rad/s   t = ${fmt(t, 2)} s${running ? '   running' : ''}`,
127	      view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: -20 });
128	    drawText(g, view, 'ΣF is the same wherever the forces act; ΣT flips when the lever arm crosses zero',
129	      view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 10, dx: 8, dy: -6 });
130	  });
131	
132	  // ---- integrate θ: one explicit Euler step of the tuple's h ----
133	  function step() {
134	    const h = store.get().h;
135	    const { alpha } = torque();
136	    const { theta, omega, t } = rot.get();
137	    rot.set({ theta: theta + h * omega, omega: omega + h * alpha, t: t + h });
138	  }
139	  const offFrame = loop.onFrame(dt => {
140	    if (!running) return;
141	    carry += dt;
142	    const h = store.get().h;
143	    let n = Math.min(Math.floor(carry / h), 200);
144	    carry -= n * h;
145	    while (n-- > 0) step();
146	  });
147	
148	  const runBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button', 'aria-pressed': 'false' }, 'Run');
149	  runBtn.addEventListener('click', () => { running = !running; carry = 0; runBtn.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(running)); runBtn.textContent = running ? 'Pause' : 'Run'; stage.invalidate(); }, { signal });
150	  const stepBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button' }, 'Step');
151	  stepBtn.addEventListener('click', step, { signal });
152	  const resetBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button' }, 'Reset');
153	  resetBtn.addEventListener('click', () => rot.set({ theta: 0, omega: 0, t: 0 }), { signal });
154	
155	  root.append(controls(el('div', { class: 'transport' }, el('div', { class: 'transport-group' }, runBtn, stepBtn, resetBtn))));
156	
157	  // ---- live math: the rotation store drives the integration, the scene drives the forces ----
158	  const derive = () => {
159	    const { sum, I, T, alpha } = torque();
160	    return { I, T, alpha, Sx: sum[0], Sy: sum[1], lever: rot.get().lever };
161	  };
162	  bindMath(article, rot, derive, { signal });
163	  bindMath(article, scene, derive, { signal });
164	  const offScrub = bindScrub(article, rot, { signal });
165	
166	  const unsubRot = rot.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
167	  const unsubScene = scene.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
168	  return {
169	    pause() { running = false; runBtn.setAttribute('aria-pressed', 'false'); runBtn.textContent = 'Run'; },
170	    destroy() { offFrame(); unsubRot(); unsubScene(); offScrub(); },
171	  };
172	}

// Panel 2, left: vectors alone, the step down from the spine — the same picture with the
// force models taken away, so both arrows are free.
//
// `a` runs from the origin; `b` runs from a's tip, so the drawing *is* the decomposition and
// `a + b` is the arrow from the origin to b's tip. Drag either tip, or scrub any of the four
// components in the equation. The dashed copies (b from the origin, a from b's tip) close the
// parallelogram: head to tail either way lands on the same sum.
//
// The four components live in a pane-local store, not the scene: a force can only be scaled
// along the direction its model gives it (gravity has one parameter and always points at the
// attractor), and a vector refresher needs two arrows that turn freely. Nothing here writes
// the scene or the tuple.

import { fragment } from 'shared/dom.js';
import { createStore } from 'shared/state.js';
import { createStage } from 'shared/gfx/stage.js';
import { createDragHandles } from 'shared/gfx/drag.js';
import { cssVar, makeView, drawGrid, drawPolyline, drawText } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
import { bindScrub } from 'shared/ui/scrub.js';
import { bindMath } from 'shared/ui/livemath.js';
import { SUM_COLOR, IDENTITY_MAP, drawForceArrow, mag } from './arrows.js';

const A_COLOR = '--accent';
const B_COLOR = '--region-edge';
const HALF_W = 4;              // the view never gets smaller than this
const REACH = 3.6;             // a drag cannot put either tip outside this box
const A_COMP = [-REACH, REACH];        // a runs from the origin, so its components reach as far
const B_COMP = [-2 * REACH, 2 * REACH];  // b runs from a's tip: corner to corner is twice that

/** a and b, tail to tip: the defaults put both arrows and their sum well inside the view. */
const START = { ax: 2.5, ay: 1.5, bx: 1, by: -3 };

const num = key => `<mn data-var="${key}" data-scrub="${key}" data-digits="2">0</mn>`;
const pair = (x, y) => `<mo>(</mo>${num(x)}<mo>,</mo>${num(y)}<mo>)</mo>`;
const sum = `<mo>(</mo><mn data-var="sx" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn data-var="sy" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>)</mo>`;

const EQUATIONS = `
<math display="block"><mrow>
  <mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo>${pair('ax', 'ay')}<mo>+</mo>${pair('bx', 'by')}<mo>=</mo>${sum}
</mrow></math>
<math display="block"><mrow>
  <mo>|</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn data-var="la" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mspace width="1em"/>
  <mo>|</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn data-var="lb" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mspace width="1em"/>
  <mo>|</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn data-var="ls" data-digits="2">0</mn>
  <mo>≤</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn data-var="lab" data-digits="2">0</mn>
</mrow></math>`;

/**
 * A vector's name beside the middle of its shaft, pushed off along the left normal. Tip
 * labels would collide here: b's tip and the sum's tip are the same point.
 */
function labelShaft(g, view, from, d, text, color) {
  const l = mag(d);
  if (l < 0.3) return;
  const nx = -d[1] / l, ny = d[0] / l;   // the plot-space left normal; Y is flipped in pixels
  drawText(g, view, text, from[0] + d[0] / 2, from[1] + d[1] / 2,
    { color: cssVar(color), size: 12, align: 'center', dx: nx * 14, dy: 4 - ny * 14 });
}

export function mount(root, ctx) {
  const { signal } = ctx;
  const article = root.closest('article') ?? root;
  let view = null, frozenHalf = null;

  const vecs = createStore({ ...START }, {
    limits: { ax: A_COMP, ay: A_COMP, bx: B_COMP, by: B_COMP },
    validate: () => undefined,
    presets: {},
  });
  const parts = () => {
    const { ax, ay, bx, by } = vecs.get();
    return { a: [ax, ay], b: [bx, by], s: [ax + bx, ay + by] };
  };
  /**
   * The view only ever grows past HALF_W, and only because a scrub can push a component past
   * REACH — a dragged tip is clamped inside it, so the plane never moves under the pointer.
   * It is frozen for the length of a drag anyway, in case a scrub already grew it.
   */
  const halfNow = ({ a, b, s }) => frozenHalf ?? Math.max(HALF_W, 1.12 * Math.max(...[a, b, s].flat().map(Math.abs)));

  const stage = createStage(root, { layers: ['plane'], aspect: 'square', signal });
  root.append(fragment(EQUATIONS));

  stage.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
    const { a, b, s } = parts();
    const g = stage.ctx('plane');
    view = makeView({ w, h, dpr, halfW: halfNow({ a, b, s }) });
    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
    drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 'x', yLabel: 'y' });

    // the parallelogram: each arrow copied to the other's tail, dashed — head to tail either
    // way lands on the same sum
    const dim = cssVar('--tick');
    drawPolyline(g, view, [0, b[0]], [0, b[1]], { color: dim, width: 1, dash: [4, 4] });
    drawPolyline(g, view, [b[0], s[0]], [b[1], s[1]], { color: dim, width: 1, dash: [4, 4] });
    // components of the sum, dropped to the axes
    drawPolyline(g, view, [s[0], s[0]], [0, s[1]], { color: cssVar(SUM_COLOR), width: 1, dash: [2, 3], alpha: 0.6 });
    drawPolyline(g, view, [0, s[0]], [s[1], s[1]], { color: cssVar(SUM_COLOR), width: 1, dash: [2, 3], alpha: 0.6 });

    // the decomposition itself: a from the origin, b from a's tip, the sum closing them.
    // b and a + b share a tip, so the names go beside each shaft, not at the point.
    drawForceArrow(g, view, [0, 0], a, IDENTITY_MAP, { color: A_COLOR, width: 2.5 });
    drawForceArrow(g, view, a, b, IDENTITY_MAP, { color: B_COLOR, width: 2.5 });
    drawForceArrow(g, view, [0, 0], s, IDENTITY_MAP, { color: SUM_COLOR, width: 3.5, head: 10 });
    labelShaft(g, view, [0, 0], a, 'a', A_COLOR);
    labelShaft(g, view, a, b, 'b', B_COLOR);
    labelShaft(g, view, [0, 0], s, 'a + b', SUM_COLOR);

    drawText(g, view, 'drag either tip', view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: -8 });
  });

  createDragHandles(stage.canvas('plane'), {
    signal, hitRadius: 14,
    handles: () => {
      const { a, s } = parts();
      return [{ id: 'a', x: a[0], y: a[1] }, { id: 'b', x: s[0], y: s[1] }];
    },
    view: () => view ?? makeView({ w: 1, h: 1, dpr: 1, halfW: HALF_W }),
    onStart: () => { frozenHalf = view?.xMax ?? HALF_W; },
    onMove: (id, p) => {
      const x = Math.max(-REACH, Math.min(REACH, p.x)), y = Math.max(-REACH, Math.min(REACH, p.y));
      if (id === 'a') { vecs.set({ ax: x, ay: y }); return; }
      const { ax, ay } = vecs.get();          // b's handle is its tip, a + b: b is the difference
      vecs.set({ bx: x - ax, by: y - ay });
    },
    onEnd: () => { frozenHalf = null; stage.invalidate(); },
  });

  bindScrub(article, vecs, { signal });
  bindMath(article, vecs, ({ ax, ay, bx, by }) => {
    const a = [ax, ay], b = [bx, by], s = [ax + bx, ay + by];
    return { sx: s[0], sy: s[1], la: mag(a), lb: mag(b), ls: mag(s), lab: mag(a) + mag(b) };
  }, { signal });

  const unsub = vecs.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
  return { destroy() { unsub(); } };
}
<article>
  <h2>Vectors</h2>
  <div class="prose">
    <p>A vector is a length and a direction: an arrow. On the plane it is two numbers, its
      components <math><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>,
      and its magnitude is the length of the arrow,
      <math><msqrt><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></msqrt></math>.</p>
    <p>Adding two vectors is placing the tail of one at the tip of the other; the sum runs from
      the first tail to the last tip. The components add one at a time:</p>
    <math display="block">
      <mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>)</mo>
      <mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo>
      <mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo>
      <msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow>
    </math>
    <p>Multiplying a vector by a number stretches it, or flips it if the number is negative, and
      never turns it. That is all <math><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow></math>
      does to the force sum: <math><mi>a</mi></math> is the same arrow, scaled by
      <math><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></math>.</p>
    <p>This is the spine’s picture with the force models taken away: two arrows that turn
      freely. <math><mi>b</mi></math> starts at <math><mi>a</mi></math>’s tip, so the drawing
      <em>is</em> the addition — <math><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow></math> runs
      from the origin to <math><mi>b</mi></math>’s tip. Drag either tip, or drag any of the four
      components in the equation beside the plane; the dashed copies close the parallelogram, and head to
      tail either way lands on the same sum.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="viz"></div>
</article>

     126 panels/02-newtonian-physics/arrows.js
// Shared by panel 2's three panes: the force arrows of the scene, their colors, and the map
// between force units and plot units.
//
// Two problems the map has to solve at once. Forces here span two orders of magnitude (the
// spring at x = 1 with k = 100 is ~100, gravity at the defaults is ~1, drag at v = 2 is 0.2),
// so a linear scale that fits the spring leaves the other two invisible; and the scale must
// not depend on *which* forces are switched on, or toggling one arrow off would move all the
// others. So: the reference magnitude is taken over every force, on and off, and the length
// is logarithmic in the magnitude —
//
//   len(F) = ARROW_LEN · ln(1 + |F| / F0) / ln(1 + ref / F0)
//
// which puts every arrow on screen at once while the numbers in the equations stay the truth.
// `toForce` inverts it, so a dragged tip still reads back as a force; a pane freezes the whole
// map for the duration of a drag so the tip stays under the pointer.

import { cssVar, drawArrow, drawText } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
import { MODELS, forceOf } from 'shared/math/forces.js';

/** CSS variable per force type; the sum and the acceleration have their own. */
export const FORCE_COLOR = Object.freeze({ wind: '--force-wind', gravity: '--force-gravity', drag: '--force-drag', spring: '--force-spring' });
export const SUM_COLOR = '--approx';
export const ACCEL_COLOR = '--exact';

export const mag = ([x, y]) => Math.hypot(x, y);
export const ARROW_LEN = 1.6;   // plot units for an arrow at the reference magnitude
const F0 = 0.1;                 // the knee of the log: below this, length is ~linear in |F|

/**
 * The active forces of a scene state as [{ i, type, label, F: [fx, fy], on }] (real or linear
 * per `linear`; the scene's own flag by default), plus the sum of the ones that are on.
 * With `all: true` the switched-off forces are in the list too (the sum still is not).
 */
export function forceVectors(state, { linear = state.linear, all = false } = {}) {
  const list = [];
  const sum = [0, 0];
  state.forces.forEach((f, i) => {
    if (!f.on && !all) return;
    const F = forceOf(f, state.body, { linear });
    if (f.on) { sum[0] += F[0]; sum[1] += F[1]; }
    list.push({ i, type: f.type, label: MODELS[f.type].label, F, on: f.on });
  });
  return { list, sum };
}

/**
 * arrowMap(state, { linear }) → { ref, len(F), toPlot(F), toForce(p) }
 *
 * `ref` is the largest magnitude in the picture — every force, switched on or not, the sum of
 * all of them, and that sum over m (the acceleration arrow) — so the map is a function of the
 * scene's numbers only, never of which switches are up.
 */
export function arrowMap(state, { linear = state.linear } = {}) {
  const { list } = forceVectors(state, { linear, all: true });
  const all = [0, 0];
  let ref = 1;
  for (const v of list) {
    all[0] += v.F[0]; all[1] += v.F[1];
    ref = Math.max(ref, mag(v.F));
  }
  const m = Math.max(state.body.m, 1e-9);
  ref = Math.max(ref, mag(all), mag(all) / m);
  const span = Math.log1p(ref / F0);
  const len = F => ARROW_LEN * Math.log1p(mag(F) / F0) / span;
  return {
    ref,
    len,
    /** A force as the plot-space offset from the arrow's tail. */
    toPlot(F) {
      const q = mag(F);
      if (q < 1e-12) return [0, 0];
      const l = len(F);
      return [F[0] / q * l, F[1] / q * l];
    },
    /** The inverse: a plot-space offset (a dragged tip) back as a force. */
    toForce(p) {
      const l = mag(p);
      if (l < 1e-12) return [0, 0];
      const q = F0 * Math.expm1(l / ARROW_LEN * span);
      return [p[0] / l * q, p[1] / l * q];
    },
  };
}

/**
 * The map for a pane whose arrows are already in plot units and need no compression: the
 * left pane's two free vectors, where the axes *are* the numbers.
 */
export const IDENTITY_MAP = Object.freeze({
  ref: 1,
  len: F => mag(F),
  toPlot: F => [F[0], F[1]],
  toForce: p => [p[0], p[1]],
});

/** Draw one arrow from `from` along the mapped F, with an optional label at the tip. */
export function drawForceArrow(g, view, from, F, map, { color, width = 2, label, head = 8, alpha = 1 } = {}) {
  const d = map.toPlot(F);
  const tip = [from[0] + d[0], from[1] + d[1]];
  const l = mag(d);
  if (l < 1e-3) return tip;
  g.save();
  g.globalAlpha = alpha;
  drawArrow(g, view, from[0], from[1], tip[0], tip[1], { color: cssVar(color), width, head });
  if (label && l >= 0.3) {   // an arrow too short to read gets no label, or they pile up
    const dx = d[0] >= 0 ? 8 : -8;
    drawText(g, view, label, tip[0], tip[1], { color: cssVar(color), size: 11, align: d[0] >= 0 ? 'left' : 'right', dx, dy: d[1] >= 0 ? -6 : 14 });
  }
  g.restore();
  return tip;
}

/**
 * The parameter patch that scales a force so its vector becomes F′ (a dragged tip):
 * wind takes the vector itself; gravity, drag, and spring keep their direction and scale
 * their one magnitude parameter by the projection of F′ onto it. Null when the force
 * currently has no length to scale (drag at v = 0, spring at x = 0).
 */
export function scalePatch(force, F, Fnext) {
  if (force.type === 'wind') return { fx: Fnext[0], fy: Fnext[1] };
  const len = mag(F);
  if (len < 1e-9) return null;
  const along = Math.max(0, (Fnext[0] * F[0] + Fnext[1] * F[1]) / len);
  const key = { gravity: 'G', drag: 'c', spring: 'k' }[force.type];
  return { [key]: force[key] * along / len };
}

// ---- the four forces' draggable equations, and the on/off row that shows one (the spine
// and the right pane both let the reader drag and toggle the same four forces) ----

const sc = (key, digits, log = false) => `<mn data-scrub="${key}" data-digits="${digits}"${log ? ' data-log' : ''}>0</mn>`;
const vec = (xk, yk) => `<mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn data-var="${xk}" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn data-var="${yk}" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>)</mo>`;

/**
 * Each force's model as a live MathML fragment: every parameter scrubbable (bindScrub, via
 * forceParamFacade below), the vector it produces read out at the end — Fgx/Fgy, Fdx/Fdy,
 * Fsx/Fsy, filled by whatever bindMath derive computes forceVectors' list.
 */
export const FORCE_EQUATIONS = Object.freeze({
  wind: `<math><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo>${sc('wind-fx', 1)}<mo>,</mo>${sc('wind-fy', 1)}<mo>)</mo></mrow></math>`,
  gravity: `<math><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow>${sc('G', 2)}<mo>·</mo>${sc('m1', 2)}<mo>·</mo>${sc('m2', 0, true)}</mrow><msup><mn data-scrub="r" data-digits="1" data-log>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac>${vec('Fgx', 'Fgy')}</mrow></math>`,
  drag: `<math><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo>${sc('drag-c', 2)}<mo>·</mo><mi>v</mi>${vec('Fdx', 'Fdy')}</mrow></math>`,
  spring: `<math><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo>${sc('spring-k', 0, true)}<mo>·</mo><mi>x</mi>${vec('Fsx', 'Fsy')}</mrow></math>`,
});

/**
 * One store-shaped view over every scrubbable parameter of every force, plus the body's
 * mass m1 (gravity's own equation, and any totals beside the rows): unique keys, so one
 * bindScrub over one article catches every row.
 */
export function forceParamFacade() {
  const at = type => scene.forceIndex(type);
  const map = {
    'wind-fx': [at('wind'), 'fx', FORCE_LIMITS.fx], 'wind-fy': [at('wind'), 'fy', FORCE_LIMITS.fy],
    G: [at('gravity'), 'G', FORCE_LIMITS.G], m2: [at('gravity'), 'm2', FORCE_LIMITS.m2], r: [at('gravity'), 'r', FORCE_LIMITS.r],
    'drag-c': [at('drag'), 'c', FORCE_LIMITS.c], 'spring-k': [at('spring'), 'k', FORCE_LIMITS.k],
    m1: ['body', 'm', BODY_LIMITS.m],
  };
  const get = () => {
    const s = scene.get(), o = {};
    for (const [key, [i, p]] of Object.entries(map)) o[key] = i === 'body' ? s.body.m : s.forces[i][p];
    return o;
  };
  return {
    get,
    set(patch) {
      for (const [key, v] of Object.entries(patch)) {
        if (!map[key] || !Number.isFinite(v)) continue;
        const [i, p] = map[key];
        if (i === 'body') scene.setMass(v); else scene.setForceParam(i, p, v);
      }
      return get();
    },
    subscribe: (fn, opts) => scene.subscribe(() => { const g = get(); fn(g, g); }, opts),
    limits: Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(map).map(([key, [, , lim]]) => [key, lim])),
  };
}

/**
 * One force's row: the on/off switch, its equation, and a hint that always occupies its full
 * width so toggling a force cannot itself change the row's width — pair with the .force-row /
 * .force-off-hint CSS, which reserves the row's height too.
 */
export function forceRow(f, i, { signal } = {}) {
  return el('div', { class: 'transport force-row' },
    toggleFn({
      label: f.type[0].toUpperCase() + f.type.slice(1),
      get: () => scene.get().forces[i].on, set: v => scene.toggleForce(i, v), subscribe: scene.subscribe, signal,
    }),
    fragment(FORCE_EQUATIONS[f.type]),
    el('span', { class: 'muted force-off-hint' }, 'off — not in ΣF'),
  );
}

/** Every force's row, in scene order. */
export function forceRows(signal) {
  return scene.get().forces.map((f, i) => forceRow(f, i, { signal }));
}

/** The vector each force produces, keyed for bindMath: Fgx/Fgy, Fdx/Fdy, Fsx/Fsy. */
export function forceComponents(state) {
  const { list } = forceVectors(state, { all: true });
  const at = type => list.find(v => v.type === type)?.F ?? [0, 0];
  const [Fgx, Fgy] = at('gravity'), [Fdx, Fdy] = at('drag'), [Fsx, Fsy] = at('spring');
  return { Fgx, Fgy, Fdx, Fdy, Fsx, Fsy };
}

Tool reply2026-09-03T18:39:39.387Z
1	<article>
2	  <h2>Torque and moment of inertia</h2>
3	  <div class="prose">
4	    <p>The forces on each rigid body are summed into <em>translational</em> and
5	      <em>rotational</em> sums. That is, all the forces that affect an object’s position are
6	      added together, and all the forces that affect an object’s rotation are added together.
7	      The total rotational force is called “torque”, <math><mi>T</mi></math>.</p>
8	    <math display="block">
9	      <mrow><munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>″</mo></msup>
10	      <mspace width="2em"/>
11	      <munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>I</mi><msup><mi>θ</mi><mo>″</mo></msup></mrow>
12	    </math>
13	    <p>Total translational force is just a sum of a bunch of
14	      <math><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow></math>’s affecting the second
15	      derivative of an object’s position, <math><mi>x</mi></math>. Torque is the same thing, but
16	      for rotational forces we use sort of a “mass equivalent” for rotation, called the
17	      <em>moment of inertia</em>. This is given by <math><mi>I</mi></math>, which is a number like
18	      mass. Torque affects the second derivative of a body’s rotation, commonly given by
19	      <math><mi>θ</mi></math>.</p>
20	    <p>A force makes torque only when it acts off the center of mass: the torque is the force
21	      times its lever arm, the perpendicular distance from the center to its line of action.
22	      A point mass has no <math><mi>θ</mi></math> and no lever arm, so this pane needs the rigid
23	      body from panel 1’s drill-down.</p>
24	    <p>Same forces, one more dimension. Slide the point of application across the body and watch
25	      the translational sum stay exactly where it was while the torque, and
26	      <math><msup><mi>θ</mi><mo>″</mo></msup></math>, swing with the lever arm — and change sign
27	      when <math><mi>d</mi></math> crosses zero.</p>
28	  </div>
29	  <div class="viz"></div>
30	</article>
31

Tool reply2026-09-03T18:39:50.226Z
1	// Panel 2, right: the rotational half, on the rigid body from panel 1's drill-down. The
2	// scene's forces are applied at one point of the body a lever arm d from its center; each
3	// makes a torque r × F, the torques sum to T = I θ″ with I = m (w² + h²) / 12 the moment of
4	// inertia of the rectangle, and θ is integrated locally (there is no rigid-body store; the
5	// scene holds a point mass). The translational sum does not depend on where the forces
6	// act; the torque does, which is the whole point of the lever arm d — a scrubbable number
7	// inside its own equation, "at d = …".
8	//
9	// θ, θ′, t and the lever arm live in a pane-local store so the equations under the stage can
10	// be ordinary live MathML: one bindMath over that store catches the integration, a second
11	// over the scene catches a force being toggled or dragged — here or on the spine, the same
12	// four force rows as the spine's (arrows.js's forceRows), so a force can be changed without
13	// leaving this pane while it runs.
14	
15	import { el, fmt, fragment } from 'shared/dom.js';
16	import { createStore } from 'shared/state.js';
17	import { createStage } from 'shared/gfx/stage.js';
18	import { cssVar, makeView, drawGrid, drawText } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
19	import { scene } from 'shared/scene.js';
20	import { controls } from 'shared/ui/controls.js';
21	import { bindMath } from 'shared/ui/livemath.js';
22	import { bindScrub } from 'shared/ui/scrub.js';
23	import { FORCE_COLOR, SUM_COLOR, forceVectors, arrowMap, drawForceArrow, forceParamFacade, forceRows, forceComponents } from './arrows.js';
24	
25	const BODY = { w: 1.6, h: 1 };
26	const HALF_W = 4;
27	const LEVER = [-0.8, 0.8];
28	
29	const EQUATIONS = `
30	<math display="block"><mrow>
31	  <munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo>
32	  <mi>r</mi><mo>×</mo><munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo>
33	  <mn data-var="T" data-digits="3">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mspace width="1em"/>
34	  <mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>w</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac>
35	  <mo>=</mo><mn data-var="I" data-digits="3">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mspace width="1em"/>
36	  <msup><mi>θ</mi><mo>″</mo></msup><mo>=</mo>
37	  <mfrac><mrow><munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><mi>I</mi></mfrac>
38	  <mo>=</mo><mn data-var="alpha" data-digits="3">0</mn>
39	</mrow></math>
40	<math display="block"><mrow>
41	  <munder><mo>∑</mo><mi>i</mi></munder><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo>
42	  <mo>(</mo><mn data-var="Sx" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn data-var="Sy" data-digits="2">0</mn><mo>)</mo>
43	  <mspace width="1em"/><mtext>at</mtext><mspace width="0.4em"/>
44	  <mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn data-var="lever" data-scrub="lever" data-digits="2">0</mn>
45	</mrow></math>`;
46	
47	export function mount(root, ctx) {
48	  const { store, loop, signal } = ctx;
49	  const article = root.closest('article') ?? root;
50	  const facade = forceParamFacade();
51	  let running = false, carry = 0;
52	
53	  // the body's rotation state and where the forces act: local to this pane
54	  const rot = createStore({ theta: 0, omega: 0, t: 0, lever: 0.5 }, {
55	    limits: { theta: [-Infinity, Infinity], omega: [-Infinity, Infinity], t: [0, Infinity], lever: LEVER },
56	    validate: () => undefined,
57	    presets: {},
58	  });
59	
60	  // ---- the same four force rows as the spine's: drag or toggle a force here too ----
61	  root.append(controls(forceRows(signal)));
62	
63	  const stage = createStage(root, { layers: ['plane'], aspect: 'wide', signal });
64	  root.append(fragment(EQUATIONS));
65	
66	  /** The rotational sum at the current θ. */
67	  function torque() {
68	    const s = scene.get();
69	    const { theta, lever } = rot.get();
70	    const { list, sum } = forceVectors(s);
71	    const r = [lever * Math.cos(theta), lever * Math.sin(theta)];
72	    const I = s.body.m * (BODY.w ** 2 + BODY.h ** 2) / 12;
73	    const T = r[0] * sum[1] - r[1] * sum[0];
74	    return { list, sum, r, I, T, alpha: T / I, s };
75	  }
76	
77	  stage.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
78	    const { list, sum, r, T, alpha, s } = torque();
79	    const { theta, omega, t, lever } = rot.get();
80	    const { body } = s;
81	    const map = arrowMap(s);
82	    const g = stage.ctx('plane');
83	    const view = makeView({ w, h, dpr, halfW: HALF_W });
84	    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
85	    drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 'x', yLabel: 'y' });
86	
87	    // the body, rotated by θ about its center of mass
88	    g.save();
89	    g.translate(view.X(body.x[0]), view.Y(body.x[1]));
90	    g.rotate(-theta);
91	    const pw = BODY.w * view.sx, ph = BODY.h * view.sy;
92	    g.fillStyle = cssVar('--accent-soft'); g.strokeStyle = cssVar('--accent'); g.lineWidth = 2 * dpr;
93	    g.beginPath(); g.rect(-pw / 2, -ph / 2, pw, ph); g.fill(); g.stroke();
94	    g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(0, 0); g.lineTo(pw / 2, 0); g.stroke();
95	    g.fillStyle = cssVar('--accent');
96	    g.beginPath(); g.arc(0, 0, 3 * dpr, 0, Math.PI * 2); g.fill();
97	    g.restore();
98	
99	    // the lever arm and the point of application
100	    const at = [body.x[0] + r[0], body.x[1] + r[1]];
101	    g.save();
102	    g.strokeStyle = cssVar('--fg'); g.lineWidth = 2.5 * dpr;
103	    g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(view.X(body.x[0]), view.Y(body.x[1])); g.lineTo(view.X(at[0]), view.Y(at[1])); g.stroke();
104	    g.fillStyle = cssVar('--fg');
105	    g.beginPath(); g.arc(view.X(at[0]), view.Y(at[1]), 5 * dpr, 0, Math.PI * 2); g.fill();
106	    g.restore();
107	    drawText(g, view, `r, d = ${fmt(lever, 2)}`, (body.x[0] + at[0]) / 2, (body.x[1] + at[1]) / 2, { color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 10, dx: 6, dy: -6 });
108	
109	    for (const f of list) drawForceArrow(g, view, at, f.F, map, { color: FORCE_COLOR[f.type], label: f.label });
110	    drawForceArrow(g, view, at, sum, map, { color: SUM_COLOR, width: 4, head: 11, label: 'ΣF' });
111	
112	    // the torque as an arc at the center: counter-clockwise for T > 0
113	    if (Math.abs(T) > 1e-6) {
114	      const R = 0.55 * view.sx, cx = view.X(body.x[0]), cy = view.Y(body.x[1]);
115	      const sweep = Math.min(Math.PI * 1.5, 0.4 + Math.abs(alpha) * 0.15);
116	      g.save();
117	      g.strokeStyle = g.fillStyle = cssVar('--unstable'); g.lineWidth = 2.5 * dpr;
118	      g.beginPath(); g.arc(cx, cy, R, -Math.PI / 2, -Math.PI / 2 + (T > 0 ? -sweep : sweep), T > 0); g.stroke();
119	      const end = -Math.PI / 2 + (T > 0 ? -sweep : sweep);
120	      const ex = cx + R * Math.cos(end), ey = cy + R * Math.sin(end);
121	      const tangent = end + (T > 0 ? -Math.PI / 2 : Math.PI / 2);
122	      const hd = 8 * dpr;
123	      g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(ex, ey);
124	      g.lineTo(ex - hd * Math.cos(tangent - 0.4), ey - hd * Math.sin(tangent - 0.4));
125	      g.lineTo(ex - hd * Math.cos(tangent + 0.4), ey - hd * Math.sin(tangent + 0.4));
126	      g.closePath(); g.fill();
127	      g.restore();
128	      drawText(g, view, 'T', body.x[0], body.x[1] + 0.55, { color: cssVar('--unstable'), size: 12, align: 'center', dx: T > 0 ? -14 : 14, dy: -6 });
129	    }
130	
131	    // the rotation state, and the point the lever arm is there to make
132	    drawText(g, view, `θ = ${fmt(theta, 3)} rad   θ′ = ${fmt(omega, 3)} rad/s   t = ${fmt(t, 2)} s${running ? '   running' : ''}`,
133	      view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: -20 });
134	    drawText(g, view, 'ΣF is the same wherever the forces act; ΣT flips when the lever arm crosses zero',
135	      view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 10, dx: 8, dy: -6 });
136	  });
137	
138	  // ---- integrate θ: one explicit Euler step of the tuple's h ----
139	  function step() {
140	    const h = store.get().h;
141	    const { alpha } = torque();
142	    const { theta, omega, t } = rot.get();
143	    rot.set({ theta: theta + h * omega, omega: omega + h * alpha, t: t + h });
144	  }
145	  const offFrame = loop.onFrame(dt => {
146	    if (!running) return;
147	    carry += dt;
148	    const h = store.get().h;
149	    let n = Math.min(Math.floor(carry / h), 200);
150	    carry -= n * h;
151	    while (n-- > 0) step();
152	  });
153	
154	  const runBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button', 'aria-pressed': 'false' }, 'Run');
155	  runBtn.addEventListener('click', () => { running = !running; carry = 0; runBtn.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(running)); runBtn.textContent = running ? 'Pause' : 'Run'; stage.invalidate(); }, { signal });
156	  const stepBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button' }, 'Step');
157	  stepBtn.addEventListener('click', step, { signal });
158	  const resetBtn = el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button' }, 'Reset');
159	  resetBtn.addEventListener('click', () => rot.set({ theta: 0, omega: 0, t: 0 }), { signal });
160	
161	  root.append(controls(el('div', { class: 'transport' }, el('div', { class: 'transport-group' }, runBtn, stepBtn, resetBtn))));
162	
163	  // ---- live math: the rotation store drives the integration, the scene drives the forces
164	  // and the force rows' own equations (forceComponents: Fgx/Fgy, Fdx/Fdy, Fsx/Fsy) ----
165	  const derive = () => {
166	    const { sum, I, T, alpha, s } = torque();
167	    return { ...forceComponents(s), I, T, alpha, Sx: sum[0], Sy: sum[1], lever: rot.get().lever };
168	  };
169	  bindMath(article, rot, derive, { signal });
170	  bindMath(article, scene, derive, { signal });
171	  const offScrub = bindScrub(article, rot, { signal });
172	  const offForceScrub = bindScrub(article, facade, { signal });
173	
174	  const unsubRot = rot.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
175	  const unsubScene = scene.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
176	  return {
177	    pause() { running = false; runBtn.setAttribute('aria-pressed', 'false'); runBtn.textContent = 'Run'; },
178	    destroy() { offFrame(); unsubRot(); unsubScene(); offScrub(); offForceScrub(); },
179	  };
180	}

<article>
  <h2>Lyapunov vs asymptotic</h2>
  <div class="prose">
    <p>Sweep the starting state over the whole plane of
      <math><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>
      and each point becomes an arrow: where the system carries that state next. The picture is
      a phase portrait, and “stable” comes in three flavors, named for Lyapunov.</p>
    <p><strong>Stable</strong> (in Lyapunov’s sense): for any tube of radius
      <math><mi>ε</mi></math> there is a starting distance <math><mi>δ</mi></math> such that
      neighbors starting within <math><mi>δ</mi></math> stay within <math><mi>ε</mi></math>.
      The undamped spring, <math><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></math>, is this:
      orbits circle the origin forever, never nearer and never farther.</p>
    <p><strong>Asymptotically stable</strong>: stable, and the neighbors also converge to the
      solution. The damped spring spirals in.</p>
    <p><strong>Unstable</strong>: some neighbor leaves, however close it started. Negative
      damping spirals out.</p>
    <p>The essay’s definition is the first of these, and the eigenvalue test in panel 7 gives
      the second: a strictly negative real part is asymptotic; a zero real part is the
      borderline circling case.</p>
    <p>The three portraits are side by side, each one labeled with its own
      <math><mi>c</mi></math> and Re <math><mi>λ</mi></math>. The tuple keeps
      <math><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></math>, so the third portrait negates
      <math><mi>c</mi></math> locally, the same trick the spine plays with its own damping.
      Drag <math><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn data-scrub="c" data-digits="2">0.10</mn></mrow></math>
      down toward zero and watch the two spirals flatten toward the same circling orbit.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="viz"></div>
</article>

// Panel 4, right: the whole initial-condition plane as a phase portrait, three times side by
// side: c = 0 (stable, orbits circle), c > 0 (asymptotically stable, spirals in), and c < 0
// (unstable, spirals out). The flow field is the system matrix applied at each lattice point;
// the trajectories are the closed form, which needs no integrator. The tuple clamps c ≥ 0,
// so the unstable portrait negates c in a local matrix and never writes it back.

import { fmt } from 'shared/dom.js';
import { createStage } from 'shared/gfx/stage.js';
import { cssVar, makeView, drawGrid, drawPolyline, drawPoint, drawText, drawVectorField, layoutGrid } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
import { systemMatrix, exactSolution, naturalFrequency } from 'shared/math/system.js';
import { apply } from 'shared/math/matrix2.js';
import { bindScrub } from 'shared/ui/scrub.js';

const SAMPLES = 400;
const HALF = 1.6;   // x half-range; v is scaled by ω so an undamped orbit is a circle
const STARTS = [[1, 0], [-0.5, 0.6], [0.2, -1]];   // (x₀, v₀/ω)

export function mount(root, ctx) {
  const { store, signal } = ctx;
  const article = root.closest('article') ?? root;

  const stage = createStage(root, { layers: ['plot'], aspect: 'wide', signal });

  stage.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
    const s = store.get();
    const g = stage.ctx('plot');
    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
    const omega = naturalFrequency(s.m, s.k);
    const wv = omega > 0 ? omega : 1;            // v-scale; k = 0 has no ω
    const period = omega > 0 ? 2 * Math.PI / omega : Infinity;
    const T = Math.min(6, 3 * period);
    // Re λ = −c / 2m per portrait, called out beside each one's own c
    const alpha = c => -c / (2 * s.m);
    const cases = [
      { c: 0, name: 'stable', note: `c = 0, Re λ = 0`, color: cssVar('--accent') },
      { c: s.c, name: 'asymptotically stable', note: `c = ${fmt(s.c, 2)}, Re λ = ${fmt(alpha(s.c), 3)}`, color: cssVar('--stable') },
      { c: -s.c, name: 'unstable', note: `c = ${fmt(-s.c, 2)}, Re λ = ${fmt(alpha(-s.c), 3)}`, color: cssVar('--unstable') },
    ];
    const cells = layoutGrid(w, h, 1, 3, 8 * dpr);
    cells.forEach((cell, i) => {
      const { c } = cases[i];
      const A = systemMatrix(s.m, c, s.k);
      g.save();
      g.translate(cell.x, cell.y);
      g.beginPath(); g.rect(0, 0, cell.w, cell.h); g.clip();
      const view = makeView({ w: cell.w, h: cell.h, dpr, halfW: HALF, halfH: HALF * wv * cell.h / cell.w });
      drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 'x', yLabel: 'v', ticks: 3 });
      drawVectorField(g, view, (x, v) => apply(A, [x, v]), { spacing: 20, normalize: true, maxLength: 12, alpha: 0.55 });
      for (const [x0, u0] of STARTS) {
        const sol = exactSolution({ m: s.m, c, k: s.k, x0, v0: u0 * wv });
        const xs = new Float64Array(SAMPLES), vs = new Float64Array(SAMPLES);
        for (let j = 0; j < SAMPLES; j++) { const t = T * j / (SAMPLES - 1); xs[j] = sol.x(t); vs[j] = sol.v(t); }
        drawPolyline(g, view, xs, vs, { color: cases[i].color, width: 1.75 });
        drawPoint(g, view, x0, u0 * wv, { r: 3.5, fill: cases[i].color });
      }
      drawText(g, view, cases[i].name, -HALF, view.yMax, { color: cases[i].color, size: 12, dx: 8, dy: 30 });
      drawText(g, view, cases[i].note, -HALF, view.yMax, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: 44 });
      g.restore();
    });
  });

  const offScrub = bindScrub(article, store, { signal, limits: { c: [0, 10] } });

  const unsub = store.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
  return { destroy() { unsub(); offScrub(); } };
}

Tool reply2026-09-03T18:40:19.319Z
// Panel 4, spine: the exact solution with an ε-tube around it and a handful of neighbors
// started nearby, all closed-form (no integrator exists yet). The neighbors start on a
// circle of radius ε in (x₀, v₀/ω), so each is exactly ε away at t = 0 and the tube is a
// fair test. The perturbation size is aux.epsilon, so it survives a remount.
//
// The panel's whole point is that a system can fail this test, and physical stability here
// is decided by Re λ = −c / 2m. The tuple clamps c ≥ 0 (LIMITS.c = [0, 50]) because a real
// spring's damping is never negative, so every case the reader could select used to be
// stable and the picture could never show its own opposite. Damping is therefore local to
// this pane — the same trick panel 6's spine plays with its overdamped c — in a small store
// whose limits run negative. It is seeded from the tuple's c and re-seeded whenever the
// tuple's c changes elsewhere, and it is never written back: a "what if" case is computed
// locally, so visiting this panel leaves the tuple exactly as it was found.

import { el, fmt } from 'shared/dom.js';
import { createStage } from 'shared/gfx/stage.js';
import { drawBundle } from 'shared/gfx/bundle.js';
import { cssVar, makeView, drawGrid, drawPolyline, drawBand, drawText } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
import { exactSolution, naturalFrequency } from 'shared/math/system.js';
import { createStore } from 'shared/state.js';
import { aux } from 'shared/aux.js';
import { bindMath } from 'shared/ui/livemath.js';
import { bindScrub } from 'shared/ui/scrub.js';

const SPAN = 6;         // seconds shown
const SAMPLES = 600;
const NEIGHBORS = 6;    // started at equal angles around the ε-circle
const C_RANGE = [-2, 50];   // this pane's own damping: below zero is the unstable what-if
const C_SCRUB = [-2, 10];   // the useful stretch of it, for the drag
const SLACK = 1.001;    // c = 0 rides the tube's edge by construction; don't call that an exit
const CAP = 3;          // y-range cap, in multiples of the readable early amplitude

/** This pane's own damping, in its own store so bindScrub() and subscribe() work unchanged. */
function localDamping(c0) {
  return createStore({ c: c0 }, {
    limits: { c: C_RANGE },
    validate: key => { throw new Error(`panel 4 spine: unknown local key: ${key}`); },
    presets: {},
  });
}

export function mount(root, ctx) {
  const { store, signal } = ctx;
  const article = root.closest('article') ?? root;

  // seeded from the reader's actual case; re-seeded only when the tuple's own c changes
  const local = localDamping(store.get().c);

  // one store-shaped view over this pane's two what-if numbers — local c and aux's epsilon —
  // so bindScrub can bind both from the article in a single pass, the way panels 2 and 5 bind
  // a facade over the scene. c never reaches C_RANGE's full stretch by drag; C_SCRUB narrows it
  // to the readable range the old slider used.
  const scrub = {
    get: () => ({ c: local.get().c, epsilon: aux.get().epsilon }),
    set(patch) {
      if (Number.isFinite(patch.c)) local.set({ c: patch.c });
      if (Number.isFinite(patch.epsilon)) aux.set({ epsilon: patch.epsilon });
      return scrub.get();
    },
    subscribe(fn, opts) {
      const relay = () => { const s = scrub.get(); fn(s, s); };
      const offLocal = local.subscribe(relay, opts);
      const offAux = aux.subscribe(relay, { immediate: false });
      return () => { offLocal(); offAux(); };
    },
    limits: { c: C_SCRUB, epsilon: aux.limits.epsilon },
  };

  const stage = createStage(root, { layers: ['plot'], aspect: 'wide', signal });

  const ts = new Float64Array(SAMPLES + 1);
  for (let i = 0; i <= SAMPLES; i++) ts[i] = SPAN * i / SAMPLES;
  const sample = sol => { const xs = new Float64Array(SAMPLES + 1); for (let i = 0; i <= SAMPLES; i++) xs[i] = sol.x(ts[i]); return xs; };

  // The neighbor sweep (exact[], the six perturbed curves, and the tube-escape stats) is the
  // same work the draw and the prose both need; memoized so bindMath's synchronous derive and
  // the next animation-frame draw agree, and a redraw or a re-render never repeats it twice.
  let measured = null, measureKey = '';
  function measure(sys, eps) {
    const key = `${sys.m}/${sys.c}/${sys.k}/${sys.x0}/${sys.v0}/${eps}`;
    if (key === measureKey) return measured;
    measureKey = key;
    const omega = naturalFrequency(sys.m, sys.k);
    const exact = sample(exactSolution(sys));

    // neighbors: (x₀ + ε cos φ, v₀ + ε ω sin φ); with k = 0 there is no ω, so perturb v by ε
    const series = [];
    let maxDist = 0, endDist = 0, far = 0, escapeAt = -1, escaped = 0;
    for (let j = 0; j < NEIGHBORS; j++) {
      const phi = 2 * Math.PI * j / NEIGHBORS;
      const dx = eps * Math.cos(phi), dv = eps * (omega > 0 ? omega : 1) * Math.sin(phi);
      const xs = sample(exactSolution({ ...sys, x0: sys.x0 + dx, v0: sys.v0 + dv }));
      let left = false;
      for (let i = 0; i <= SAMPLES; i++) {
        const d = Math.abs(xs[i] - exact[i]);
        if (d > maxDist) maxDist = d;
        if (i === SAMPLES && d > endDist) endDist = d;
        if (Math.abs(xs[i]) > far) far = Math.abs(xs[i]);
        if (d > eps * SLACK) {
          left = true;
          if (escapeAt < 0 || ts[i] < escapeAt) escapeAt = ts[i];
        }
      }
      if (left) escaped++;
      series.push({ xs: ts, ys: xs });
    }
    measured = { exact, series, maxDist, endDist, far, escapeAt, escaped };
    return measured;
  }

  /** The system currently drawn: the tuple's m, k, x₀, v₀ with this pane's own c. */
  const sysNow = () => ({ ...store.get(), c: local.get().c });

  stage.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
    const s = store.get();
    const c = local.get().c;                 // this pane's own damping, never the tuple's
    const sys = { ...s, c };                 // the system actually drawn
    const eps = aux.get().epsilon;
    const { exact, series, far, escapeAt } = measure(sys, eps);
    const lo = new Float64Array(SAMPLES + 1), hi = new Float64Array(SAMPLES + 1);
    for (let i = 0; i <= SAMPLES; i++) { lo[i] = exact[i] - eps; hi[i] = exact[i] + eps; }

    const g = stage.ctx('plot');
    // ampQ, the amplitude over the first quarter of the window, is the scale the reader can
    // actually read; amp is the whole window, which an unstable case blows up by e^{|c|T/2m}.
    // The view fits what it can and caps at CAP · ampQ, so a runaway leaves the top of the
    // frame instead of squashing the ε-tube to nothing — which is what leaving looks like.
    let amp = 0, ampQ = 0;
    for (let i = 0; i <= SAMPLES; i++) {
      const a = Math.abs(exact[i]);
      if (a > amp) amp = a;
      if (i <= SAMPLES / 4 && a > ampQ) ampQ = a;
    }
    const base = Math.max(ampQ + eps, 0.5);
    const yMax = 1.2 * Math.min(Math.max(amp + eps, far), CAP * base);
    const view = makeView({ w, h, dpr, xMin: 0, xMax: SPAN, yMin: -yMax, yMax });
    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
    drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 't', yLabel: 'x' });
    drawBand(g, view, ts, lo, hi, { fill: cssVar('--exact'), alpha: 0.16 });
    drawBundle(g, view, series, {
      highlight: -1,
      color: c < 0 ? cssVar('--unstable') : cssVar('--approx'),
      dimAlpha: 0.7, dimWidth: 1.25,
    });
    drawPolyline(g, view, ts, exact, { color: cssVar('--exact'), width: 2 });
    if (escapeAt >= 0) {
      const late = escapeAt > 0.62 * SPAN;
      drawPolyline(g, view, [escapeAt, escapeAt], [-yMax, yMax], { color: cssVar('--unstable'), width: 1.25, dash: [5, 4], alpha: 0.9 });
      drawText(g, view, `leaves the tube at t = ${fmt(escapeAt, 2)} s`, escapeAt, yMax, {
        color: cssVar('--unstable'), size: 11, align: late ? 'right' : 'left', dx: late ? -6 : 6, dy: 16,
      });
    }
    // anchored at t = 0: a runaway takes the tube off the top of the frame long before t = SPAN
    drawText(g, view, 'ε-tube around the exact solution', 0, exact[0] - eps, { color: cssVar('--exact'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: 16 });
  });

  const offScrub = bindScrub(article, scrub, { signal });

  // the verdict sentence in the prose: the sign of Re λ = −c / 2m, and nothing else. Bound
  // to all three stores (the tuple for m, this pane's own c, and aux's ε) so it stays live
  // whichever one moves; `measure` above is memoized, so this repeats no work the draw
  // hasn't already done for the same (m, c, k, x₀, v₀, ε).
  function verdictSlots() {
    const sys = sysNow(), eps = aux.get().epsilon;
    const { maxDist, endDist, escaped } = measure(sys, eps);
    const alpha = -sys.c / (2 * sys.m);
    const verdict = sys.c > 0 ? 'asymptotically stable'
      : sys.c < 0 ? 'unstable'
        : escaped === 0 ? 'stable' : 'unstable';
    const cls = verdict === 'unstable' ? 'unstable' : 'stable';
    return {
      alpha, maxDist, endDist,
      localc: sys.c,
      verdict: el('span', { class: cls }, verdict),
      tubeState: endDist > eps * SLACK ? 'outside' : 'inside',
    };
  }
  bindMath(article, store, verdictSlots, { signal });
  bindMath(article, local, verdictSlots, { signal });
  bindMath(article, aux, verdictSlots, { signal });

  // the tuple only re-seeds the local damping (m, k, x₀, v₀ still come from it directly)
  const unsub = store.subscribe((s, patch) => {
    if ('c' in patch) local.set({ c: s.c });
    stage.invalidate();
  }, { immediate: false });
  const unsubLocal = local.subscribe(stage.invalidate, { immediate: false });
  const unsubAux = aux.subscribe((s, patch) => { if ('epsilon' in patch) stage.invalidate(); }, { immediate: false });
  return { destroy() { unsub(); unsubLocal(); unsubAux(); offScrub(); } };
}
---HTML---
<article>
  <h2>Physical stability</h2>
  <div class="prose">
    <p>Physical stability is a statement about a solution to our system of equations. It roughly
      says that a solution is physically stable if it starts out close to the real solution and
      stays close to the real solution, <em>forever</em>.</p>
    <blockquote><p><em>“…the intuitive description is that a solution <math><mrow><mi>φ</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>
      is [physically] stable if each solution <math><mrow><mi>ψ</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>
      that starts out close to <math><mrow><mi>φ</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>
      remains close to it for all time.”</em> — <em><a
      href="https://www.sciencedirect.com/book/monograph/9781558607408/game-physics">Game
      Physics</a> by David Eberly</em></p></blockquote>
    <p>This is a property of the <em>system</em>, before any integrator is chosen. The exact
      solution is drawn with an <math><mi>ε</mi></math>-tube around it and a handful of neighbors
      started nearby. A stable system pulls the neighbors into the tube; an unstable one lets
      them leave, however close they started.</p>
    <p>So before we can answer any sort of optimization problem, we need to figure out if our
      solutions are physically stable. We can analyze this using something called Linear
      Stability Theory (LST). However, this requires us to make some concessions, which is the
      next panel.</p>
    <p>Whether the neighbors converge or leave is decided by the damping. A real spring’s
      damping is never negative, so the spring’s own <math><mi>c</mi></math> stops at zero and
      the failing case would be unreachable. The <math><mi>c</mi></math> below is therefore
      <em>this pane’s own</em>: it runs below zero into a what-if the spring itself cannot be
      put into, and it is never written back. Drag the tube’s
      <math><mrow><mo>|</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>φ</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mo>≤</mo><mi>ε</mi><mo>=</mo><mn data-scrub="epsilon" data-digits="3" data-log>0.100</mn></mrow></math>,
      then drag the damping down through zero and watch the neighbors stop converging and
      leave the tube.</p>
    <p>Right now this case is <span data-var="verdict">stable</span>, since
      <math><mrow><mi>Re</mi><mi>λ</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn data-var="localc" data-scrub="c" data-snap="0" data-digits="2">0.10</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn data-var="alpha" data-digits="3">0</mn></mrow></math>.
      The farthest any neighbor has strayed from the exact curve is
      <math><mn data-var="maxDist" data-digits="4">0</mn></math>; at
      <math><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mtext> s</mtext></mrow></math> it sits
      <math><mn data-var="endDist" data-digits="4">0</mn></math> away,
      <span data-var="tubeState">inside</span> the ε-tube.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="viz"></div>
</article>

<article>
  <h2>What “linear” means</h2>
  <div class="prose">
    <p>A function is linear when the output of a sum is the sum of the outputs, and scaling the
      input scales the output by the same amount:</p>
    <math display="block">
      <mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo>
      <mspace width="2em"/>
      <mi>f</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>s</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>·</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow>
    </math>
    <p>A spring passes: <math><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></math>,
      so stretching it twice as far pulls twice as hard, and two stretches add. So does drag,
      <math><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow></math>. A squared term fails the same check:</p>
    <math display="block">
      <mrow><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>≠</mo>
      <msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup>
      <mtext>, because </mtext>
      <msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo>
      <msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow>
    </math>
    <p>That cross term is what makes <math><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></math>
      gravity non-linear, and it is why the sum of forces on the spine can collapse to three
      coefficients only after every force has been made linear. A sum of linear functions is
      linear; add one squared term and the whole sum stops being so.</p>
    <p>Pick two inputs and compare the two sides of the check for the spring, then for the
      squared term.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="viz"></div>
</article>

Tool reply2026-09-03T18:41:35.345Z
// Panel 5, left: what linear means. One function f, two draggable inputs a and b, and the
// check f(a + b) = f(a) + f(b): a, b, and a + b sit on the x axis with their outputs dropped
// to the curve, and the arithmetic is called out in the corner of the plot. f(x) = 2x
// passes; f(x) = x² fails by exactly 2ab.

import { el, fmt } from 'shared/dom.js';
import { createStage } from 'shared/gfx/stage.js';
import { createDragHandles } from 'shared/gfx/drag.js';
import { cssVar, makeView, drawGrid, drawPolyline, drawPoint, drawText } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
import { controls } from 'shared/ui/controls.js';

const HALF_W = 3.2;
const FUNCTIONS = {
  linear: { label: 'f(x) = 2x', f: x => 2 * x },
  square: { label: 'f(x) = x²', f: x => x * x },
};

export function mount(root, ctx) {
  const { signal } = ctx;
  let kind = 'linear';
  let a = 0.8, b = 1.3;
  let view = null;

  const stage = createStage(root, { layers: ['plot'], aspect: 'wide', signal });

  stage.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
    const { f } = FUNCTIONS[kind];
    const g = stage.ctx('plot');
    const yMax = kind === 'square' ? 8 : 6.5;
    view = makeView({ w, h, dpr, xMin: -HALF_W, xMax: HALF_W, yMin: -yMax * 0.45, yMax });
    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
    drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 'x', yLabel: 'f(x)' });

    const xs = [], ys = [];
    for (let i = 0; i <= 200; i++) { const x = -HALF_W + 2 * HALF_W * i / 200; xs.push(x); ys.push(f(x)); }
    drawPolyline(g, view, xs, ys, { color: cssVar('--fg'), width: 1.5 });

    const fa = f(a), fb = f(b), fab = f(a + b), sum = fa + fb;
    const blue = cssVar('--exact'), green = cssVar('--stable'), red = cssVar('--approx');
    // drops from the three points to the axes
    for (const [x, y, color] of [[a, fa, blue], [b, fb, green], [a + b, fab, red]]) {
      drawPolyline(g, view, [x, x], [0, y], { color, width: 1, dash: [3, 3], alpha: 0.6 });
      drawPolyline(g, view, [x, 0], [y, y], { color, width: 1, dash: [3, 3], alpha: 0.6 });
      drawPoint(g, view, x, y, { r: 5, fill: color });
    }
    // handles on the axis
    drawPoint(g, view, a, 0, { r: 7, fill: blue });
    drawPoint(g, view, b, 0, { r: 7, fill: green });
    drawPoint(g, view, a + b, 0, { r: 5, fill: red, stroke: '#fff' });
    drawText(g, view, 'a', a, 0, { color: blue, size: 12, align: 'center', dy: 22 });
    drawText(g, view, 'b', b, 0, { color: green, size: 12, align: 'center', dy: 22 });
    drawText(g, view, 'a + b', a + b, 0, { color: red, size: 12, align: 'center', dy: -12 });

    // the arithmetic, called out top-right, clear of the grid's own labels
    const passes = Math.abs(sum - fab) < 1e-9;
    const corner = { align: 'right', dx: -8 };
    drawText(g, view, `f(a) + f(b) = ${fmt(fa, 2)} + ${fmt(fb, 2)} = ${fmt(sum, 2)}`, HALF_W, yMax, { ...corner, color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 12, dy: 16 });
    drawText(g, view, `f(a + b) = f(${fmt(a + b, 2)}) = ${fmt(fab, 2)}`, HALF_W, yMax, { ...corner, color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 12, dy: 32 });
    drawText(g, view, passes ? 'equal: linear' : `off by ${fmt(fab - sum, 2)}: not linear`, HALF_W, yMax,
      { ...corner, color: cssVar(passes ? '--stable' : '--unstable'), size: 12, dy: 48 });
  });

  createDragHandles(stage.canvas('plot'), {
    signal, hitRadius: 14,
    handles: () => [{ id: 'a', x: a, y: 0 }, { id: 'b', x: b, y: 0 }],
    view: () => view ?? makeView({ w: 1, h: 1, dpr: 1, xMin: -HALF_W, xMax: HALF_W, yMin: -1, yMax: 1 }),
    onMove: (id, p) => {
      const x = Math.max(-HALF_W + 0.1, Math.min(HALF_W - 0.1, p.x));
      if (id === 'a') a = x; else b = x;
      stage.invalidate();
    },
  });

  const buttons = Object.entries(FUNCTIONS).map(([k, v]) => el('button', { class: 'btn', type: 'button', 'aria-pressed': String(k === kind) }, v.label));
  buttons.forEach((btn, i) => btn.addEventListener('click', () => {
    kind = Object.keys(FUNCTIONS)[i];
    buttons.forEach((x, j) => x.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(i === j)));
    stage.invalidate();
  }, { signal }));
  root.append(controls(el('div', { class: 'transport' }, el('div', { class: 'transport-group' }, ...buttons))));

  return { destroy() {} };
}

Tool reply2026-09-03T18:42:26.770Z
<article>
  <h2>Taylor expansion and 1/r² gravity</h2>
  <div class="prose">
    <p>Any other forces (like perhaps magnetism or lift on an airfoil) can be linearized by using
      something called a Taylor series expansion. This creates an infinite series, from which we
      can take the lower-order terms and then simply discard the non-linear terms.</p>
    <p>Around an operating distance <math><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>, with
      <math><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></math>:</p>
    <math display="block">
      <mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo>
      <mfrac><mn>1</mn><msubsup><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mfrac>
      <mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>δ</mi></mrow><msubsup><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>3</mn></msubsup></mfrac>
      <mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>δ</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><msubsup><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>4</mn></msubsup></mfrac>
      <mo>−</mo><mo>…</mo></mrow>
    </math>
    <p>Keep the first term and gravity is a constant, <math><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></math>.
      Keep two and it is linear in <math><mi>δ</mi></math>. Everything after that is what the
      linear model throws away, and it is small only while <math><mi>δ</mi></math> is small.</p>
    <p>Slide the number of terms kept and watch the series close on the real curve. The pale
      dashed line is <math><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>, the operating point the
      expansion is built around; the dark one is where the mass actually is. Push the mass away
      from <math><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> on the spine — or drag
      <math><mi>r</mi></math> itself down inside gravitation’s equation there — and the kept
      terms peel away from the truth. That gap is what the linear model does not know about:
      the details that have fallen through the cracks of the abstraction.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="viz"></div>
</article>

Tool reply2026-09-03T18:42:27.924Z
// Panel 5, right: the Taylor expansion of 1/r² about the gravity force's operating distance
// r₀, every term count in the sweep drawn at once against the true curve, with the term-count
// strip under the graph selecting which one is highlighted (aux.highlight, so the choice
// survives a remount). r₀ and the mass's distance come from the scene the spine sets up.

import { el } from 'shared/dom.js';
import { createStage } from 'shared/gfx/stage.js';
import { drawBundle } from 'shared/gfx/bundle.js';
import { cssVar, makeView, drawGrid, drawPolyline, drawText } from 'shared/gfx/plot2d.js';
import { inverseSquareExpansion } from 'shared/math/taylor.js';
import { sweep, sweepKey } from 'shared/math/sweep.js';
import { scene } from 'shared/scene.js';
import { aux } from 'shared/aux.js';
import { sweepStrip } from 'shared/ui/sweep.js';

const TERMS = [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6];   // degrees kept; 0 is the constant m g keeps
const CURVE = 240;

const highlightIndex = () => Math.min(TERMS.length - 1, Math.max(0, aux.get().highlight < 0 ? 1 : aux.get().highlight));

/** What keeping this many terms buys, in one line under the curve. */
const verdict = hi => (hi === 0 ? 'a constant: m g' : hi === 1 ? 'linear in δ: all a linear model keeps' : 'past δ¹: what linearizing drops');

export function mount(root, ctx) {
  const { signal } = ctx;
  const gi = scene.forceIndex('gravity');

  const taylor = createStage(root, { layers: ['plot'], aspect: 'square', signal });
  taylor.onDraw(({ w, h, dpr }) => {
    const r0 = scene.get().forces[gi].r;
    const hi = highlightIndex();
    const g = taylor.ctx('plot');
    const yMax = 3.2 / (r0 * r0);
    const view = makeView({ w, h, dpr, xMin: 0.3 * r0, xMax: 2 * r0, yMin: -0.25 * yMax, yMax });
    g.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
    drawGrid(g, view, { xLabel: 'r', yLabel: '1 / r²', ticks: 4 });
    const rs = [], truth = [];
    for (let i = 0; i <= CURVE; i++) { const r = view.xMin + (view.xMax - view.xMin) * i / CURVE; rs.push(r); truth.push(1 / (r * r)); }
    const series = sweep(TERMS, n => { const e = inverseSquareExpansion(r0, n); return rs.map(r => e.evaluate(r)); }, { key: sweepKey({ panel: '5-right', r0 }) })
      .map(({ result }) => ({ xs: rs, ys: result }));
    drawBundle(g, view, series, { highlight: hi, color: cssVar('--approx'), width: 2.25, dimWidth: 1, dimAlpha: 0.25 });
    drawPolyline(g, view, rs, truth, { color: cssVar('--exact'), width: 2 });
    drawPolyline(g, view, [r0, r0], [view.yMin, view.yMax], { color: cssVar('--muted'), width: 1, dash: [4, 4] });
    const dist = r0 + scene.get().body.x[1];
    drawPolyline(g, view, [dist, dist], [view.yMin, view.yMax], { color: cssVar('--fg'), width: 1.5, dash: [2, 3] });
    drawText(g, view, 'r₀', r0, view.yMax, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 11, dx: 4, dy: 14 });
    drawText(g, view, 'the mass', dist, view.yMax, { color: cssVar('--fg'), size: 11, dx: 4, dy: 28 });
    drawText(g, view, verdict(hi), view.xMin, view.yMin, { color: cssVar('--muted'), size: 11, dx: 8, dy: -10 });
  });

  const strip = sweepStrip({
    values: TERMS, label: 'terms kept', format: n => `${n + 1} term${n ? 's' : ''}`, signal, initial: highlightIndex(),
    onSelect: i => aux.set({ highlight: i }),
  });
  root.append(el('div', { class: 'controls' }, strip.el));

  const unsub = scene.subscribe(() => taylor.invalidate(), { immediate: false });
  const unsubAux = aux.subscribe((s, patch) => { if ('highlight' in patch) { strip.select(highlightIndex(), { notify: false }); taylor.invalidate(); } }, { immediate: false });
  return { destroy() { unsub(); unsubAux(); } };
}
